1	import logging
2	import os
3	import shutil
4	import sqlite3
5	from contextlib import contextmanager
6	from typing import Any, Tuple
7	
8	from backend.infrastructure.database_jobs import DatabaseJobsMixin
9	from backend.infrastructure.database_media import DatabaseMediaMixin
10	from backend.infrastructure.database_user_models import DatabaseUserModelsMixin
11	
12	logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
13	
14	# The highest schema version this binary knows how to migrate TO (the top of the migration ladder
15	# in `_init_db`). PACKAGING_PLAN.md P2d uses it for the upgrade-safety contract on a downloadable,
16	# auto-updating app: refuse to open a DB from a NEWER app (downgrade guard) and back the DB up before
17	# an upgrade migration runs. `tests/test_database.py::test_schema_version_matches_ladder` pins it to
18	# the ladder so adding a `_migrate_to_vN` without bumping this fails CI.
19	SCHEMA_VERSION = 9
20	
21	
22	class SchemaTooNewError(RuntimeError):
23	    """The on-disk DB was created by a newer app version than this binary understands (P2d)."""
24	
25	
26	class Database(DatabaseMediaMixin, DatabaseJobsMixin, DatabaseUserModelsMixin):
27	    def __init__(self, db_path: str):
28	        self.db_path = db_path
29	        self._init_db()
30	
31	    @contextmanager
32	    def _connect(self):
33	        """Transaction scope that also CLOSES the connection.
34	
35	        ``with sqlite3.connect(...)`` alone is only a commit/rollback scope — it
36	        never closes, so every call leaked its connection to GC (lingering file
37	        handles on the WAL sidecars under Windows). Internal methods use this.
38	        """
39	        conn = self._get_connection()
40	        try:
41	            with conn:
42	                yield conn
43	        finally:
44	            conn.close()
45	
46	    def _get_connection(self):
47	        # busy_timeout: wait (not fail) on a locked DB — needed once the async job model
48	        # adds concurrent writers (a swallowed 'database is locked' silently drops segments).
49	        conn = sqlite3.connect(self.db_path, timeout=30.0)
50	        conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
51	        # Enforce ON DELETE CASCADE / SET NULL — SQLite leaves FKs off per-connection.
52	        conn.execute("PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON")
53	        # WAL allows concurrent readers during a write; busy_timeout bounds lock waits.
54	        conn.execute("PRAGMA busy_timeout = 30000")
55	        try:
56	            conn.execute("PRAGMA journal_mode = WAL")
57	        except sqlite3.OperationalError:
58	            pass  # e.g. a read-only/networked FS that can't do WAL — degrade gracefully
59	        return conn
60	
61	    def _execute_write(self, sql: str, params: Tuple[Any, ...], error_msg: str) -> bool:
62	        """Run ONE bool-returning single-statement write inside a connection scope.
63	
64	        Captures the connect/commit/except-logger.error/return-False boilerplate shared by
65	        the bool-returning single-statement writers. ``error_msg`` is the caller's exact
66	        per-method message (already formatted with its own ids) — logged verbatim on a
67	        swallowed write fault so each writer keeps its specific log text. Returns True on
68	        success, False (after logging ``error_msg``) on any exception."""
69	        try:
70	            with self._connect() as conn:
71	                conn.cursor().execute(sql, params)
72	                conn.commit()
73	            return True
74	        except Exception as e:
75	            logger.error(f"{error_msg}: {e}")
76	            return False
77	
78	    @staticmethod
79	    def _add_column_idempotent(cursor: sqlite3.Cursor, ddl: str) -> None:
80	        """Apply a bare ``ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN`` migration step re-runnably.
81	
82	        A DDL ``ALTER`` auto-commits independently of the surrounding ``with conn:``
83	        transaction, so an interrupted v3/v4 block can leave the column added while
84	        ``user_version`` stays un-bumped — and the next open would re-run the same
85	        ``ALTER`` and raise ``OperationalError: duplicate column name``, bricking every
86	        future ``Database(...)`` construction (``_init_db`` runs in ``__init__``). Swallow
87	        ONLY that specific error so the ladder stays crash-safe, matching the re-runnable
88	        ``CREATE ... IF NOT EXISTS`` v1/v2/v5 blocks.
89	        """
90	        try:
91	            cursor.execute(ddl)
92	        except sqlite3.OperationalError as exc:
93	            if "duplicate column name" not in str(exc).lower():
94	                raise
95	
96	    def _init_db(self):
97	        """Initializes tables for videos, play_segments, and events."""
98	        with self._connect() as conn:
99	            cursor = conn.cursor()
100	
101	            self._create_base_tables(cursor)
102	
103	            # Versioned migrations live here. PRAGMA user_version is the schema
104	            # contract — bump it for every change and add an ordered `if version < N`
105	            # block below. Tests assert the expected version, so accidental drift fails CI.
106	            version = cursor.execute("PRAGMA user_version").fetchone()[0]
107	
108	            # P2d upgrade-safety (no-op for a fresh DB or one already at SCHEMA_VERSION):
109	            # (1) DOWNGRADE GUARD — a DB from a NEWER app (version > what we know) has schema this
110	            #     binary can't reason about; opening it could corrupt data, so refuse loudly.
111	            if version > SCHEMA_VERSION:
112	                raise SchemaTooNewError(
113	                    f"This database (schema v{version}) was created by a newer version of the app "
114	                    f"than this one (knows up to v{SCHEMA_VERSION}). Upgrade the app, or point it at "
115	                    f"a different data directory (RALLY_APP_HOME). DB: {self.db_path}"
116	                )
117	            # (2) BACKUP-BEFORE-MIGRATE — an EXISTING older DB is about to be upgraded in place; a
118	            #     failed migration could corrupt it, so snapshot it first (best-effort: WARN, never
119	            #     block the upgrade). version 0 = a brand-new DB → nothing to back up.
120	            if 0 < version < SCHEMA_VERSION:
121	                self._backup_before_migrate(conn, version)
122	
123	            if version < 1:
124	                self._migrate_to_v1(cursor)
125	
126	            if version < 2:
127	                self._migrate_to_v2(cursor)
128	
129	            if version < 3:
130	                self._migrate_to_v3(cursor)
131	
132	            if version < 4:
133	                self._migrate_to_v4(cursor)
134	
135	            if version < 5:
136	                self._migrate_to_v5(cursor)
137	
138	            if version < 6:
139	                self._migrate_to_v6(cursor)
140	
141	            if version < 7:
142	                self._migrate_to_v7(cursor)
143	
144	            if version < 8:
145	                self._migrate_to_v8(cursor)
146	
147	            if version < 9:
148	                self._migrate_to_v9(cursor)
149	            # future: if version < 10: cursor.execute("ALTER TABLE ..."); PRAGMA user_version = 10
150	
151	            conn.commit()
152	
153	    def _backup_before_migrate(
154	        self, conn: sqlite3.Connection, from_version: int
155	    ) -> None:
156	        """Snapshot the DB to ``<db>.bak-v<from_version>`` before an upgrade migration (P2d).
157	
158	        Checkpoints the WAL into the main file first so the copy is complete. Best-effort: a backup
159	        failure WARNS but does NOT block the upgrade (the ladder is itself crash-safe; refusing to
160	        start because a backup couldn't be written would be worse than proceeding without one)."""
161	        backup = f"{self.db_path}.bak-v{from_version}"
162	        try:
163	            conn.execute("PRAGMA wal_checkpoint(FULL)")
164	            shutil.copy2(self.db_path, backup)
165	            logger.info(
166	                "[DB] backed up v%s database before upgrading → %s",
167	                from_version,
168	                backup,
169	            )
170	        except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — the backup is a best-effort safety net; its failure
171	            # must NEVER block the in-place upgrade (the migration ladder is itself crash-safe). Log loud.
172	            logger.warning(
173	                "[DB] could not back up the database before migrating from v%s (%s); "
174	                "proceeding with the in-place upgrade.",
175	                from_version,
176	                exc,
177	            )
178	
179	    def backup_database(self, dest: str) -> str:
180	        """Write a consistent copy of the DB to ``dest`` (an 'export my data' affordance, P2d).
181	        Checkpoints the WAL first so ``dest`` is self-contained. Returns ``dest``."""
182	        parent = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(dest))
183	        if parent:
184	            os.makedirs(parent, exist_ok=True)
185	        with self._connect() as conn:
186	            conn.execute("PRAGMA wal_checkpoint(FULL)")
187	        shutil.copy2(self.db_path, dest)
188	        return dest
189	
190	    def _create_base_tables(self, cursor):
191	        """Create the videos / play_segments / events base tables (idempotent)."""
192	        # Videos Table
193	        cursor.execute("""
194	            CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS videos (
195	                id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
196	                filepath TEXT NOT NULL,
197	                processed_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP,
198	                status TEXT NOT NULL,
199	                validation_results TEXT
200	            )
201	        """)
202	
203	        # Play Segments Table (Extensible metadata JSON)
204	        cursor.execute("""
205	            CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS play_segments (
206	                id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
207	                video_id TEXT NOT NULL,
208	                start_time REAL NOT NULL,
209	                end_time REAL NOT NULL,
210	                duration REAL NOT NULL,
211	                server TEXT,
212	                receiver TEXT,
213	                metadata TEXT,
214	                FOREIGN KEY (video_id) REFERENCES videos(id) ON DELETE CASCADE
215	            )
216	        """)
217	
218	        # Events Table
219	        cursor.execute("""
220	            CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS events (
221	                id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
222	                segment_id INTEGER NOT NULL,
223	                timestamp REAL NOT NULL,
224	                type TEXT NOT NULL,
225	                player TEXT,
226	                details TEXT,
227	                FOREIGN KEY (segment_id) REFERENCES play_segments(id) ON DELETE CASCADE
228	            )
229	        """)
230	
231	    def _migrate_to_v1(self, cursor):
232	        # v1: raw candidate detections (pre-confirmation), detector-agnostic.
233	        # Common fields are columns; detector-specific metrics go in `stats` JSON,
234	        # so a new segmenter reuses this table by setting its own `detector`/`stats`.
235	        cursor.execute("""
236	            CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS candidate_segments (
237	                id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
238	                video_id TEXT NOT NULL,
239	                detector TEXT NOT NULL,
240	                chunk_index INTEGER,
241	                start_time REAL NOT NULL,
242	                end_time REAL NOT NULL,
243	                duration REAL NOT NULL,
244	                confidence REAL,
245	                stats TEXT,
246	                detection_params TEXT,
247	                confirmed_segment_id INTEGER,
248	                human_label TEXT,
249	                notes TEXT,
250	                created_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP,
251	                FOREIGN KEY (video_id) REFERENCES videos(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
252	                FOREIGN KEY (confirmed_segment_id) REFERENCES play_segments(id) ON DELETE SET NULL
253	            )
254	        """)
255	        cursor.execute("""
256	            CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_candidates_video_detector
257	                ON candidate_segments(video_id, detector)
258	        """)
259	        cursor.execute("PRAGMA user_version = 1")
260	
261	    def _migrate_to_v2(self, cursor):
262	        # v2: async job model (DEFERRED_HARDENING_PLAN #16). One row per submitted
263	        # /api/process request. `status` is the EXECUTION state of the job
264	        # (queued|running|done|failed); the pipeline's own verdict (e.g. a video
265	        # that failed validation) lives inside `result` JSON as result["status"].
266	        # `result` is the full sync-era response dict (incl. report paths), so the
267	        # observability report is the job's artifact, per the plan.
268	        cursor.execute("""
269	            CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS jobs (
270	                id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
271	                video_path TEXT NOT NULL,
272	                video_id TEXT,
273	                segmenter TEXT,
274	                player_pool TEXT,
275	                max_frames INTEGER,
276	                status TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'queued',
277	                progress TEXT,
278	                error TEXT,
279	                result TEXT,
280	                created_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP,
281	                started_at TIMESTAMP,
282	                finished_at TIMESTAMP
283	            )
284	        """)
285	        cursor.execute("CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_jobs_status ON jobs(status)")
286	        cursor.execute("PRAGMA user_version = 2")
287	
288	    def _migrate_to_v3(self, cursor):
289	        # v3: self-scheduling execution policy (EXECUTION_POLICY.md P2). `policy` = the
290	        # JSON ExecutionPolicy a job carries; `next_poll_at` = when a 'waiting' job is due
291	        # to resume. A 'waiting' status + due-time lets a job SELF-SCHEDULE — any worker
292	        # re-claims it via `claim_due_job` when due — with NO master node: the table IS
293	        # the coordinator. ALTER (not recreate) so existing v2 job rows survive; the
294	        # ADD COLUMNs are applied idempotently so an interrupted upgrade can re-run them.
295	        self._add_column_idempotent(cursor, "ALTER TABLE jobs ADD COLUMN policy TEXT")
296	        self._add_column_idempotent(
297	            cursor, "ALTER TABLE jobs ADD COLUMN next_poll_at TIMESTAMP"
298	        )
299	        cursor.execute(
300	            "CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_jobs_due ON jobs(status, next_poll_at)"
301	        )
302	        cursor.execute("PRAGMA user_version = 3")
303	
304	    def _migrate_to_v4(self, cursor):
305	        # v4: personalization groundwork (PERSONALIZATION_PLAN.md §2). Add a NULLABLE
306	        # `user_id` to videos + candidate_segments so a future per-user / multi-tenant
307	        # path can scope rows to an owner. **NULL = local install = byte-identical to
308	        # today** — every existing row stays NULL and every existing query (which never
309	        # filters on user_id) is unaffected. ALTER (not recreate) so v1-v3 rows survive;
310	        # the ADD COLUMNs are applied idempotently so an interrupted upgrade can re-run them.
311	        # Additive ONLY: no served-behavior change rides on this slice.
312	        self._add_column_idempotent(
313	            cursor, "ALTER TABLE videos ADD COLUMN user_id TEXT"
314	        )
315	        self._add_column_idempotent(
316	            cursor, "ALTER TABLE candidate_segments ADD COLUMN user_id TEXT"
317	        )
318	        # Partial indexes (WHERE user_id IS NOT NULL) keep the NULL=local fast path free
319	        # of index churn while making the eventual per-user lookups cheap.
320	        cursor.execute(
321	            "CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_videos_user ON videos(user_id) "
322	            "WHERE user_id IS NOT NULL"
323	        )
324	        cursor.execute(
325	            "CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_candidates_user "
326	            "ON candidate_segments(user_id) WHERE user_id IS NOT NULL"
327	        )
328	        cursor.execute("PRAGMA user_version = 4")
329	
330	    def _migrate_to_v5(self, cursor):
331	        # v5: per-user model store (PERSONALIZATION_PLAN.md §2). One row per (user_id,
332	        # version): the resolved per-user `fusion_config` overlay + an INLINE
333	        # `classifier_json` (tenant-safe — no file artifact), plus the promotion evidence
334	        # and provenance that earned it. `is_active` pins the one row the serving bridge
335	        # resolves (the FUTURE owner-gated wiring into `_select_for_handoff`). This slice
336	        # only persists/loads it — NOTHING reads it on the served path yet.
337	        #
338	        #   user_id              — owner of the model (NULL allowed = the local install)
339	        #   version              — monotonic per-user model version
340	        #   baseline_version     — the shared baseline this was fit against (upgrade switch)
341	        #   feature_schema_hash  — fingerprint of the feature set; MINOR-vs-MAJOR re-fit gate
342	        #   fusion_config        — JSON per-user FusionConfig overlay (cue_flags / thresholds)
343	        #   classifier_json      — JSON LogisticRegression.to_dict() (None = config-only model)
344	        #   promotion_evidence   — JSON per_user_gate PromotionDecision (why it was promoted)
345	        #   n_videos_at_fit      — held-out-user corpus size when fit (min_n trust floor input)
346	        #   is_active            — the one resolved row for this user (partial-unique below)
347	        cursor.execute("""
348	            CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS user_models (
349	                id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
350	                user_id TEXT,
351	                version INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 1,
352	                baseline_version TEXT,
353	                feature_schema_hash TEXT,
354	                fusion_config TEXT,
355	                classifier_json TEXT,
356	                promotion_evidence TEXT,
357	                n_videos_at_fit INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
358	                is_active INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
359	                created_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP
360	            )
361	        """)
362	        # One model version per user (NULLs compare distinct in SQLite, so the local
363	        # install can still carry multiple versioned rows; the active-row guard below
364	        # is what enforces a single served model).
365	        cursor.execute("""
366	            CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_user_models_user_version
367	                ON user_models(user_id, version)
368	        """)
369	        # At most ONE active model per user: a partial-unique index over the active rows.
370	        # (SQLite treats NULL user_id rows as distinct, so the local install's single
371	        # active row is still guarded — there is one local user.)
372	        cursor.execute("""
373	            CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_user_models_one_active
374	                ON user_models(user_id) WHERE is_active = 1
375	        """)
376	        cursor.execute("PRAGMA user_version = 5")
377	
378	    def _migrate_to_v6(self, cursor):
379	        # v6: per-job COST LEDGER (COMPUTE_DECOUPLED_SERVING M8, D8). NULLABLE cost columns on
380	        # `jobs` (NULL = no cost recorded = byte-identical to today; nothing records until
381	        # billing.enabled + a real pricebook) + a VERSIONED `pricebook` table. Cost is stored in
382	        # USD (matches GCP billing = one source of truth); INR is a display conversion at a
383	        # configurable FX rate. ALTER (not recreate) so v1-v5 job rows survive; ADD COLUMNs are
384	        # idempotent so an interrupted upgrade can re-run them. Additive ONLY — no served change.
385	        for col, typ in (
386	            ("owner_id", "TEXT"),  # who pays (the non-owner user / tenant)
387	            ("compute_backend", "TEXT"),  # local_gpu | cloud_run | ...
388	            ("gpu_type", "TEXT"),  # L4 | T4 | ... (selects the per-second GPU rate)
389	            ("gpu_active_seconds", "REAL"),
390	            ("wall_seconds", "REAL"),
391	            ("bytes_out", "INTEGER"),  # egress
392	            ("gemini_cost_usd", "REAL"),  # provider-reported, already USD
393	            ("pricebook_version", "TEXT"),
394	            ("cost_usd", "REAL"),  # computed total (USD = source of truth)
395	            (
396	                "cost_breakdown_json",
397	                "TEXT",
398	            ),  # {gpu_seconds: .., egress_gb: .., gemini_usd: ..}
399	        ):
400	            self._add_column_idempotent(
401	                cursor, f"ALTER TABLE jobs ADD COLUMN {col} {typ}"
402	            )
403	        # Partial index keeps the NULL=no-cost fast path churn-free while making per-owner billing cheap.
404	        cursor.execute(
405	            "CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_jobs_owner ON jobs(owner_id) "
406	            "WHERE owner_id IS NOT NULL"
407	        )
408	        # Versioned pricebook: one row per (version, resource, gpu_type). Rates change + differ by
409	        # gpu_type → re-version (never mutate a shipped version, so a recorded cost stays auditable).
410	        cursor.execute("""
411	            CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS pricebook (
412	                version TEXT NOT NULL,
413	                resource TEXT NOT NULL,
414	                gpu_type TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT '',
415	                unit_price_usd REAL NOT NULL DEFAULT 0.0,
416	                currency TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'USD',
417	                effective_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP
418	            )
419	        """)
420	        cursor.execute(
421	            "CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_pricebook_key "
422	            "ON pricebook(version, resource, gpu_type)"
423	        )
424	        # Seed a PLACEHOLDER version with every rate 0.0 → cost_usd computes to 0 (nothing
425	        # user-facing changes). The owner INSERTs a real, calibrated version (real GCP $/GPU-s,
426	        # egress $/GB) and flips billing.pricebook_version to it — never overwrite this seed.
427	        for resource, gpu_type in (
428	            ("gpu_seconds", "L4"),
429	            ("gpu_seconds", "T4"),
430	            ("wall_seconds", ""),
431	            ("egress_gb", ""),
432	        ):
433	            cursor.execute(
434	                "INSERT OR IGNORE INTO pricebook (version, resource, gpu_type, unit_price_usd, "
435	                "currency) VALUES ('placeholder-v0', ?, ?, 0.0, 'USD')",
436	                (resource, gpu_type),
437	            )
438	        cursor.execute("PRAGMA user_version = 6")
439	
440	    def _migrate_to_v7(self, cursor):
441	        # v7: record the UI LOCALE a job was submitted in (the language the SPA was rendered in,
442	        # e.g. "kn", "hi-IN"). NULLABLE — NULL = unrecorded = byte-identical to today (CLI / older
443	        # clients / pre-v7 rows). Enables PMF analytics ("which language markets use the tool",
444	        # count_jobs_by_locale) and pairs with the localized reel watermark (the SPA sends the same
445	        # locale to /api/compile). Additive-only ALTER; idempotent so an interrupted upgrade re-runs.
446	        self._add_column_idempotent(cursor, "ALTER TABLE jobs ADD COLUMN locale TEXT")
447	        cursor.execute("PRAGMA user_version = 7")
448	
449	    def _migrate_to_v8(self, cursor):
450	        # v8: record the per-request COMPUTE choice the SPA compute-picker sent ("local" forces the
451	        # in-process segmenter; "cloud" forces a Cloud Run dispatch). It must survive the async
452	        # submit→worker boundary — the worker reconstructs the ProcessRequest from this row, so a
453	        # NULL/unset value (CLI / older clients / pre-v8 rows) means "use the server default", i.e.
454	        # byte-identical to today. Additive-only ALTER; idempotent so an interrupted upgrade re-runs.
455	        self._add_column_idempotent(cursor, "ALTER TABLE jobs ADD COLUMN compute TEXT")
456	        cursor.execute("PRAGMA user_version = 8")
457	
458	    def _migrate_to_v9(self, cursor):
459	        # v9: the jobs table now holds TWO kinds of work — `kind`='process' (the original, NULL ⇒
460	        # process for pre-v9 rows) and `kind`='compile' (the async reel stitch, #329; /api/compile
461	        # is now 202+poll like /api/process so a slow ffmpeg stitch can't trip the edge 524). `params`
462	        # is the kind-specific JSON payload a compile job carries (segment_ids/output_filename/locale)
463	        # since those don't map onto the process columns. Additive-only ALTERs; idempotent.
464	        self._add_column_idempotent(cursor, "ALTER TABLE jobs ADD COLUMN kind TEXT")
465	        self._add_column_idempotent(cursor, "ALTER TABLE jobs ADD COLUMN params TEXT")
466	        cursor.execute("PRAGMA user_version = 9")
467	
468	
469	    # Domain CRUD methods live in mixins so Database remains the public facade while
470	    # schema/migration/connection logic stays centralized in this module.
471

31	# --- media metadata --------------------------------------------------------
32	def video_meta_from_context(
33	    val_context: Optional[Dict[str, Any]], video_path: Optional[str]
34	) -> Dict[str, Any]:
35	    """Derive VideoMeta fields from the validators' shared ffprobe_meta. fps_source
36	    distinguishes a real probe from the absence of one (the fallback footgun)."""
37	    meta: Dict[str, Any] = {"fps_source": "unknown"}
38	    if video_path and os.path.exists(video_path):
39	        try:
40	            meta["size_mb"] = round(os.path.getsize(video_path) / (1024 * 1024), 1)
41	        except OSError:
42	            pass
43	
44	    ffmeta = (val_context or {}).get("ffprobe_meta") or {}
45	    streams = ffmeta.get("streams") or []
46	    fmt = ffmeta.get("format") or {}
47	    if streams:
48	        s = streams[0]
49	        w, h = s.get("width"), s.get("height")
50	        meta["width"], meta["height"] = w, h
51	        if w and h:
52	            meta["resolution"] = f"{w}x{h}"
53	        fps_str = s.get("r_frame_rate", "0/1")
54	        try:
55	            num, den = (int(x) for x in fps_str.split("/"))
56	            if den:
57	                meta["fps"] = round(num / den, 2)
58	                meta["fps_source"] = "probed"
59	        except (ValueError, ZeroDivisionError):
60	            pass
61	        if s.get("codec_name"):
62	            meta["container"] = fmt.get("format_name") or s.get("codec_name")
63	    if fmt.get("duration"):
64	        try:
65	            meta["duration_s"] = round(float(fmt["duration"]), 1)
66	        except (ValueError, TypeError):
67	            pass
68	    if fmt.get("format_name"):
69	        meta["container"] = fmt.get("format_name")
70	
71	    # Audio readiness signal (for Input Quality UX extension per review item 2).
72	    # Guidelines make "record with audio on, mic unobstructed" a first-class requirement
73	    # (ADR-007) because shuttle thwacks are a strong rally cue for recall.
74	    # We only record presence here (cheap, from ffprobe already in context); the actual
75	    # thwack onset probe (pipeline/audio/hit_detector) remains a diagnostic / calibration
76	    # tool for now.
77	    audio_streams = [s for s in streams if (s or {}).get("codec_type") == "audio"]
78	    meta["audio_present"] = len(audio_streams) > 0
79	    return meta
80	
81	
82	# --- stage builders --------------------------------------------------------
83	def _validation_stage(rec: "obsreport.RunRecorder", val_results: List[Any]) -> None:
84	    with rec.stage("validation") as st:
85	        total = len(val_results)
86	        passed = sum(1 for r in val_results if getattr(r, "passed", False))
87	        st.add_metric("checks_passed", passed)
88	        st.add_metric("checks_total", total)
89	        failures = [r for r in val_results if not getattr(r, "passed", True)]
90	        for r in failures:
91	            st.messages.append(
92	                f"FAILED {getattr(r, 'validator_name', '?')}: {getattr(r, 'message', '')}"
93	            )
94	        st.status = "error" if failures else ("ok" if total else "not_run")
95	
96	
97	def _segmentation_stage(
98	    rec: "obsreport.RunRecorder",
99	    play_segments: List[Dict[str, Any]],
100	    candidates: List[Dict[str, Any]],
101	    segmenter_requested: Optional[str],
102	    segmenter_actual: Optional[str],
103	    config_default: Optional[str],
104	    was_reingest: bool,
105	    ran: bool,
106	) -> None:
107	    with rec.stage("segmentation") as st:
108	        if not ran:
109	            st.status = "not_run"

43	    def __init__(self, *, video_id="vidCloud", returncode=0, rows=None):

46	        self.rows = (

53	        self.calls.append(list(argv))
54	        out_uri = argv[argv.index("--out-uri") + 1].rstrip("/")
55	        done_uri = argv[argv.index("--done-uri") + 1]
56	        if self.returncode == 0:
57	            outputs = os.path.join(out_uri, "outputs", self.video_id)

60	                json.dump(

68	            with open(

75	                w.writerow(["start", "end", "shot_count", "ending_reason"])

78	        os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(done_uri), exist_ok=True)

84	                    "segment_count": len(self.rows),
85	                    "status": "success",
86	                },
87	                f,
88	            )
89	        return "exec-1"

309	def test_migration_idempotent_on_interrupted_upgrade(tmp_path):
310	    """An INTERRUPTED v3/v4 upgrade must not brick the DB. A DDL ``ALTER`` auto-commits
311	    on its own, so a crash after the ADD COLUMNs but before the ``PRAGMA user_version``
312	    bump leaves the columns present while the version is stale — re-opening re-runs the
313	    bare ALTERs. The ladder must tolerate that (re-runnable, like the v1/v2/v5 blocks)
314	    instead of raising ``OperationalError: duplicate column name``."""
315	    db_path = tmp_path / "interrupted.db"
316	    Database(
317	        str(db_path)
318	    )  # fully migrate to the current version (v3/v4 columns all present)
319	
320	    # Simulate the interruption: the v3/v4 ADD COLUMNs already landed and auto-committed,
321	    # but the process died before the version bump, so user_version is stuck at 2.
322	    conn = sqlite3.connect(str(db_path))
323	    conn.execute("DROP TABLE IF EXISTS user_models")  # a true v2 DB lacks the v5 table

326	    conn.close()

331	    # INSERT OR IGNORE), and must reach v9.
332	    Database(str(db_path))
333	    conn = sqlite3.connect(str(db_path))
334	    assert conn.execute("PRAGMA user_version").fetchone()[0] == 9
335	    conn.close()

### 84. user (2026-07-07T17:09:59.265Z)

1	"""Lightweight Result type for standardizing success vs. failure across the pipeline.
2	
3	This is the start of the Error/Result Contract standardization (low-regret item #3).
4	
5	The goal is to make it impossible to confuse "no data / no rallies" with "something actually failed"
6	(e.g. missing API key, WSL healthcheck failure, provider error, etc.).
7	
8	We use simple dataclasses so callers can use isinstance checks or structural pattern matching.
9	This is intentionally small and incremental — we start by applying it to the segmenter
10	and provider layers (the biggest sources of silent [] returns today).
11	
12	The types are generic where it makes sense. Callers can still access .value on Success
13	or .message on Failure.
14	
15	This module has no dependencies on the rest of the app.
16	"""
17	
18	from __future__ import annotations
19	
20	from dataclasses import dataclass, field
21	from typing import Any, Generic, List, Optional, Tuple, TypeVar, Union
22	
23	T = TypeVar("T")
24	
25	
26	@dataclass
27	class Success(Generic[T]):
28	    """Represents a successful outcome with an optional list of non-fatal warnings."""
29	
30	    value: T
31	    warnings: List[str] = field(default_factory=list)
32	
33	    def is_success(self) -> bool:
34	        return True
35	
36	    def unwrap(self) -> T:
37	        """Return the value. Only safe to call if you already know it's a Success."""
38	        return self.value
39	
40	
41	@dataclass
42	class Failure:
43	    """Represents a failure with a human-readable message and optional details."""
44	
45	    message: str
46	    details: Optional[dict[str, Any]] = None
47	    is_recoverable: bool = False  # e.g. missing API key (recoverable) vs internal crash
48	
49	    def is_success(self) -> bool:
50	        return False
51	
52	    def unwrap(self) -> Any:
53	        """Raise to make misuse obvious in code that assumes success."""
54	        raise RuntimeError(f"Attempted to unwrap a Failure: {self.message}")
55	
56	
57	# Convenient alias for use in type hints: Result[list[dict], Failure]
58	# Result[ValueType] means Success[ValueType] | Failure
59	# (the Failure "error" type is fixed for this contract)
60	Result = Union[Success[T], Failure]
61	
62	
63	def unwrap_or_failure(result: Any) -> Tuple[Optional[Any], Optional[Failure]]:
64	    """Split a segmenter/provider return into ``(value, failure)`` so a caller branches once.
65	
66	    - ``Failure``            -> ``(None, failure)``
67	    - ``Success(value=v)``   -> ``(v, None)``
68	    - a bare value (legacy code that predates this contract and still returns a raw list)
69	      -> ``(value, None)``
70	
71	    This is the same isinstance/``.value`` unwrap that ``backend/main.py`` does inline; callers
72	    that forget it (e.g. ``len(process_video(...))``) crash with ``TypeError: ... has no len()``
73	    on a ``Success`` dataclass. ``result`` is typed ``Any`` because the contract is heterogeneous
74	    (Success | Failure | legacy bare value)."""
75	    if isinstance(result, Failure):
76	        return None, result
77	    value = result.value if hasattr(result, "value") else result
78	    return value, None
79

### 88. assistant (2026-07-07T17:12:08.561Z)

"""Validation-result cache keyed by video_id (task 4585aa00).

``video_id`` is the MD5 of the file bytes (backend/utils/hashing.py), so an identical-content
REUPLOAD keys the same cache row. When that content was already validated under the SAME validator
configuration, ``_execute_processing`` reuses the stored verdict instead of re-running the
(minutes-long on a heavy 4K clip) validator suite. This is distinct from the pre-existing
whole-pipeline cache, which only short-circuits on a prior SUCCESSFUL segmentation — so a reupload
after a SEGMENTATION FAILURE used to re-pay the full validation from scratch (the 2026-07-07 CUJ).

These tests drive ``_execute_processing`` with a stubbed validator suite (a spy that COUNTS how many
times it actually runs) + a stubbed segmenter, so the assertions are on validation-run count, not on
real decoding. Cardinal guarantees exercised: same content + same config → validators run ONCE across
two uploads; a validation-config change forces a re-run; ``force=true`` bypasses; a cached FAIL is
replayed without a re-decode; a different content never reuses another video's verdict.
"""

import logging

import backend.main as m
from backend.config.models import AppConfig
from backend.infrastructure.database import Database
from backend.pipeline.validators.base import ValidationResult, registry
from backend.results import Failure

_ORCH_LOGGER = "backend.orchestration"


def _pass():
    return [ValidationResult(validator_name="x", passed=True, message="ok", details={})]


def _fail():
    return [
        ValidationResult(
            validator_name="blur_check",
            passed=False,
            message="too blurry",
            details={},
        )
    ]


class _ValSpy:
    """Stands in for ``registry.run_all_with_context`` and counts real invocations.

    A cache HIT must NOT call this — ``calls`` staying flat across a second upload is the proof that
    validation was skipped (the whole point of the cache)."""

    def __init__(self, results):
        self._results = results
        self.calls = 0

    def __call__(self, *a, **k):
        self.calls += 1
        return list(self._results), {}


class _FailSeg:
    """A segmenter whose detection FAILS (Failure result) — reproduces "validation passed, detection
    failed → no cached segments", the exact state the whole-pipeline cache can't short-circuit."""

    def __init__(self, db_, cfg_):
        pass

    def process_video(self, *a, **k):
        return Failure("detector crashed")


class _OkSeg:
    """A segmenter that writes one play row (never reached when validation fails first)."""

    def __init__(self, db_, cfg_):
        self.db = db_

    def process_video(self, vid, path, *a, **k):
        sid = self.db.add_play_segment(vid, 0.0, 5.0)
        return [
            {
                "id": sid,
                "start_time": 0.0,
                "end_time": 5.0,
                "duration": 5.0,
                "metadata": {},
            }
        ]


def _setup(tmp_path, monkeypatch, *, results, seg, cfg=None, content=b"identical-bytes"):
    """Wire the module globals + a real (temp-file) DB + stubbed validator/segmenter, and return
    ``(db, spy, video_path)``. The video file's BYTES fix its video_id, so writing the same bytes
    twice (a 'reupload') hits the same cache key."""
    db = Database(str(tmp_path / "t.db"))
    monkeypatch.setattr(m, "db_instance", db)
    monkeypatch.setattr(m, "global_config", cfg or AppConfig())
    spy = _ValSpy(results)
    monkeypatch.setattr(m.registry, "run_all_with_context", spy)
    monkeypatch.setattr(m.segmenter_registry, "get", lambda name: seg)
    video = tmp_path / "v.mp4"
    video.write_bytes(content)
    return db, spy, str(video)


def _process(video, **kw):
    return m._execute_processing(m.ProcessRequest(video_path=video, segmenter="motion", **kw))


# --- the CUJ: reupload after a segmentation failure must NOT re-validate --------------------------


def test_reupload_after_segmentation_failure_skips_validation(tmp_path, monkeypatch, caplog):
    """Validation PASSES but detection FAILS → no segments, so the whole-pipeline cache misses on the
    reupload. The validation cache must still short-circuit: validators run exactly ONCE across the two
    uploads (the second reuses the cached PASS and goes straight to segmentation)."""
    db, spy, video = _setup(tmp_path, monkeypatch, results=_pass(), seg=_FailSeg)

    out1 = _process(video)
    assert out1["status"] == "failed"  # detection failed
    assert spy.calls == 1  # validated once
    vid = out1["video_id"]
    cached = db.get_validation_cache(vid)
    assert cached is not None and cached["passed"] is True  # verdict persisted
    assert db.query_play_segments(vid, {}) == []  # no segments → whole-pipeline cache WILL miss

    with caplog.at_level(logging.INFO, logger=_ORCH_LOGGER):
        out2 = _process(video)
    assert out2["status"] == "failed"
    assert out2["video_id"] == vid  # same content id
    assert spy.calls == 1  # validators did NOT re-run — the cached verdict was reused
    assert any(
        "Reusing cached validation verdict" in r.getMessage() for r in caplog.records
    )


def test_cached_row_records_the_current_fingerprint(tmp_path, monkeypatch):
    """The persisted verdict is tagged with the config fingerprint it was computed under, so a later
    lookup can tell 'same config' from 'config changed'."""
    db, spy, video = _setup(tmp_path, monkeypatch, results=_pass(), seg=_FailSeg)
    out = _process(video)
    cached = db.get_validation_cache(out["video_id"])
    assert cached["fingerprint"] == registry.config_fingerprint(m.global_config)


# --- guardrails: config change + force must both re-validate --------------------------------------


def test_config_change_forces_revalidation(tmp_path, monkeypatch):
    """Changing a validation threshold flips the fingerprint, so the stale verdict is NOT reused — the
    validators run again under the new config (never serve a PASS decided under different thresholds)."""
    db, spy, video = _setup(tmp_path, monkeypatch, results=_pass(), seg=_FailSeg)
    _process(video)
    assert spy.calls == 1
    m.global_config.validation.min_blur_threshold = 999.0  # different validator config
    _process(video)
    assert spy.calls == 2  # re-validated under the changed config


def test_sport_change_forces_revalidation(tmp_path, monkeypatch):
    """``sport`` is not under ``validation`` but the relevance validator keys its court-color profile
    off it, so it is part of the fingerprint — switching sport re-validates."""
    db, spy, video = _setup(tmp_path, monkeypatch, results=_pass(), seg=_FailSeg)
    _process(video)
    assert spy.calls == 1
    m.global_config.sport = "tennis"
    _process(video)
    assert spy.calls == 2


def test_force_bypasses_validation_cache(tmp_path, monkeypatch):
    """``force=true`` must re-validate even with an identical cached verdict (mirrors how it bypasses
    the whole-pipeline segment cache)."""
    db, spy, video = _setup(tmp_path, monkeypatch, results=_pass(), seg=_FailSeg)
    _process(video)
    assert spy.calls == 1
    _process(video, force=True)
    assert spy.calls == 2  # forced re-validation despite the cache


def test_force_refreshes_the_cached_verdict(tmp_path, monkeypatch):
    """A forced re-validation re-writes the cache under the current fingerprint, so a subsequent
    (non-forced) reupload reuses the FRESH verdict rather than re-decoding again."""
    db, spy, video = _setup(tmp_path, monkeypatch, results=_pass(), seg=_FailSeg)
    _process(video)
    _process(video, force=True)
    assert spy.calls == 2
    _process(video)  # non-forced: should hit the refreshed cache
    assert spy.calls == 2


# --- a cached FAILURE is reused too (no re-decode of a known-bad clip) -----------------------------


def test_cached_validation_failure_is_reused(tmp_path, monkeypatch):
    """A clip that FAILED validation caches its failure verdict; a reupload replays the SAME failure
    without re-decoding (the whole-pipeline cache never fires — the video never became 'processed')."""
    db, spy, video = _setup(tmp_path, monkeypatch, results=_fail(), seg=_OkSeg)
    out1 = _process(video)
    assert out1["status"] == "failed" and "too blurry" in out1["message"]
    assert spy.calls == 1
    out2 = _process(video)
    assert out2["status"] == "failed"
    assert out2["message"] == out1["message"]  # identical failure replayed
    assert spy.calls == 1  # not re-decoded


# --- content-hash keying: a different video never reuses another's verdict -------------------------


def test_different_content_does_not_reuse_verdict(tmp_path, monkeypatch):
    """Even with the same filename, DIFFERENT bytes → a different video_id → its own validation. A
    cached verdict never leaks across distinct content."""
    db, spy, video = _setup(tmp_path, monkeypatch, results=_pass(), seg=_FailSeg)
    _process(video)
    assert spy.calls == 1
    other = tmp_path / "other.mp4"
    other.write_bytes(b"totally-different-bytes")
    _process(str(other))
    assert spy.calls == 2  # a different content hash never reuses another video's verdict


# --- the fingerprint itself: stable, and sensitive to every verdict-affecting knob ----------------


def test_config_fingerprint_is_stable_and_config_sensitive():
    base = AppConfig()
    fp = registry.config_fingerprint(base)
    assert fp == registry.config_fingerprint(AppConfig())  # deterministic across instances
    assert len(fp) == 64  # sha256 hex digest

    c_blur = AppConfig()
    c_blur.validation.min_blur_threshold += 1
    c_fps = AppConfig()
    c_fps.validation.min_fps += 1
    c_res = AppConfig()
    c_res.validation.min_resolution_height += 1
    c_scene = AppConfig()
    c_scene.validation.max_scene_cuts_per_minute += 1
    c_pts = AppConfig()
    c_pts.validation.pts_max_gap_seconds += 1
    c_rel = AppConfig()
    c_rel.validation.relevance.court_pixels_min_ratio = 0.9
    c_sport = AppConfig()
    c_sport.sport = "tennis"

    fps = {
        registry.config_fingerprint(c)
        for c in (base, c_blur, c_fps, c_res, c_scene, c_pts, c_rel, c_sport)
    }
    # Every distinct config (incl. sport + each validation threshold) yields a distinct fingerprint.
    assert len(fps) == 8


def test_config_fingerprint_accepts_dict_config():
    """Robust to a dict config (not only a typed AppConfig) so the fingerprint never crashes the
    pipeline on a legacy caller."""
    typed = AppConfig()
    as_dict = typed.model_dump(mode="json")
    # Same content via dict vs typed still produces a valid 64-char digest (value need not match the
    # typed one — dict lacks the model coercion — but it must be stable + non-crashing).
    d_fp = registry.config_fingerprint(as_dict)
    assert isinstance(d_fp, str) and len(d_fp) == 64
    assert d_fp == registry.config_fingerprint(dict(as_dict))

+    def _migrate_to_v10(self, cursor):
+        # v10: validation-result CACHE keyed by the content id. A video's id IS the MD5 of its bytes
+        # (backend/utils/hashing.py::compute_video_id), so a REUPLOAD of identical content keys the
+        # same row — and when that content was ALREADY validated under the SAME validator config, the

+        # suite (minutes on a heavy 4K clip on the CPU-only control-plane; the 2026-07-07 CUJ re-paid it
+        # on every retry after a segmentation failure). Columns:
+        #   fingerprint — ValidationRegistry.config_fingerprint (the validation.* config + sport +
+        #                 validator set + a schema version); a lookup reuses the verdict ONLY on a
+        #                 fingerprint match, so a config/sport/validator change misses → re-validates.
+        #   passed      — the overall verdict (1/0), so a cached FAIL short-circuits without a decode.
+        #   results     — the JSON ValidationResult list, replayed into the response + report.
+        # Standalone table (NO foreign key): the row is written right after validation runs — which can
+        # be BEFORE the videos UPSERT exists — and is dropped explicitly by clear_video_data. UPSERT on
+        # the video_id PK keeps ONE (latest) verdict per video. Additive-only — nothing pre-existing
+        # changes; an install that never reuploads behaves byte-identically to v9.
+        cursor.execute("""
+            CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS validation_cache (
+                video_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
+                fingerprint TEXT NOT NULL,
+                passed INTEGER NOT NULL,
+                results TEXT NOT NULL,
+                created_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP
+            )
+        """)
+        cursor.execute("PRAGMA user_version = 10")

+        segmentation and a cached FAIL returns the same failure — in both cases without re-decoding the

+        with self._connect() as conn:

+                    "WHERE video_id = ?",
+                    (video_id,),
+                )
+                .fetchone()
+            )
+            if not row:
+                return None
+            d = dict(row)
+            d["passed"] = bool(d["passed"])
+            d["results"] = json.loads(d["results"] or "[]")
+            return d

+            validation = config.get("validation")
+        validation_repr = (
+            validation.model_dump(mode="json")
+            if hasattr(validation, "model_dump")
+            else validation
+        )
+        sport: Any = getattr(config, "sport", None)
+        if sport is None and isinstance(config, dict):
+            sport = config.get("sport")
+        payload = {
+            "schema": _VALIDATION_FINGERPRINT_SCHEMA,
+            "validators": sorted(v.name for v in self._validators),
+            "sport": sport,
+            "validation": validation_repr,
+        }
+        blob = json.dumps(
+            payload, sort_keys=True, default=str, separators=(",", ":")
+        )
+        return hashlib.sha256(blob.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()
+
 
 # Shared global registry
 registry = ValidationRegistry()

### 154. assistant (2026-07-07T17:21:56.763Z)

## What & why

Adds a **validation-result cache keyed by `video_id`** so a reupload of identical content does **not** re-run the validator suite when that content was already validated under the same validator configuration.

Today the only skip path in `_execute_processing` is the whole-pipeline cache, which fires only when the video is `status=="processed"` **and** has cached play segments (i.e. a prior *successful* segmentation). So after a **segmentation failure** (validation passed, detection failed → no cached segments), a reupload of the same `video_id` re-runs the full validator suite from scratch — minutes on a heavy clip on the CPU-only control-plane (~6.5 min on the 5.3K 10-bit HEVC "Video 1"). This bit the owner repeatedly during the 2026-07-07 CUJ retries.

`video_id` is the MD5 of the file bytes (`compute_video_id`), so identical content → identical key. This PR reuses the stored verdict when the request's `video_id` already has one computed under the **same validator-config fingerprint**: PASS → straight to segmentation; FAIL → return the stored failure — in both cases **without re-decoding the video**.

Composes with #513 (worker already skips its own re-validation on cloud dispatch) and the shared-decode speedup (#512): together, a post-failure reupload reaches segmentation in **~0 validation time**.

## Design

- **Config fingerprint** — `ValidationRegistry.config_fingerprint(config)` (`backend/pipeline/validators/base.py`): SHA-256 over
  - the **whole `validation` config block** (min_blur_threshold / min_fps / min_resolution / max_scene_cuts_per_minute / pts gap / relevance court-color bounds) — hashing the full model, not a hand-picked subset, so a **new validation knob is covered automatically**;
  - **`sport`** — the relevance validator keys its court-color profile off `config.sport`, a top-level field *outside* `validation`, so switching sports must invalidate;
  - the **sorted validator names** — adding/removing a validator changes which checks run;
  - a **schema version** (`_VALIDATION_FINGERPRINT_SCHEMA`) — manual escape hatch for validator *logic* changes the config can't express.
- **Storage** — a dedicated `validation_cache` table (**migration v10**), keyed by `video_id`, columns `fingerprint / passed / results / created_at`. Chosen over extending the video record so there's exactly **one read site and one write site** — no threading the fingerprint through `add_video`'s four callers or the cloud-staging path. `set/get_validation_cache` in `database_media.py`; `clear_video_data` drops the cache row too.
- **Wiring** (`backend/orchestration.py`, between the whole-pipeline cache and the validator run): fingerprint match → replay the stored verdict; a config/sport/validator change misses → re-validate + rewrite; **`force=true` bypasses** the reuse and refreshes the verdict (mirrors how it bypasses the pipeline cache). A cache-read fault degrades to a normal validation.

**Guardrails honored:** never serve a stale PASS/FAIL (any threshold/sport/validator change flips the fingerprint → re-validate); content-hash keying means a different video (even same filename) never reuses another's verdict. On a reuse the observability report's media block degrades gracefully to size-only (no fresh ffprobe_meta) — the deliberate cost of skipping the decode; segmentation reads neither, so it is unaffected.

## Tests

New `tests/test_validation_cache.py` (10 tests), driving `_execute_processing` with a validator spy that counts real runs:
- **the CUJ** — reupload after a segmentation failure validates **once** across two uploads (asserted via run count **and** the reuse log line);
- config change and sport change each **force re-validation**; `force=true` bypasses; a forced run **refreshes** the cache;
- a cached **FAIL** is replayed without a re-decode; **different content** never reuses a verdict;
- fingerprint is stable + sensitive to every verdict-affecting knob, and accepts a dict config.

Plus a DB round-trip/UPSERT/clear test in `test_database.py`, and the schema-version-ladder asserts bumped 9→10 (`test_database.py`, `test_async_jobs.py`, `test_job_worker_loop.py`, `test_locale_analytics.py`).

## Verification (local, matching CI)

- **Full suite** (`python run_all_tests.py --cov=backend --cov=training --cov-report=term --cov-fail-under=70`): _(filled in below)_
- **ruff** `ruff check .` (0.15.16): clean on all changed files. (Pre-existing 18 errors are only in the untracked dev tools `score_local.py` / `score_golden_gate.py`, not in this change or the committed tree.)
- **mypy** `mypy backend training` (2.1.0): **Success, no issues found in 149 source files.**

Related: task "Speed up video validation" (#512/#514), #513.

# /code-review

> If you see unfamiliar placeholders or need to check which tools are connected, see [CONNECTORS.md](../../CONNECTORS.md).

Review code changes with a structured lens on security, performance, correctness, and maintainability.

## Usage

```
/code-review <PR URL or file path>
```

Review the provided code changes: @518

If no specific file or URL is provided, ask what to review.

## How It Works

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                      CODE REVIEW                                   │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│  STANDALONE (always works)                                       │
│  ✓ Paste a diff, PR URL, or point to files                      │
│  ✓ Security audit (OWASP top 10, injection, auth)               │
│  ✓ Performance review (N+1, memory leaks, complexity)           │
│  ✓ Correctness (edge cases, error handling, race conditions)    │
│  ✓ Style (naming, structure, readability)                        │
│  ✓ Actionable suggestions with code examples                    │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│  SUPERCHARGED (when you connect your tools)                      │
│  + Source control: Pull PR diff automatically                    │
│  + Project tracker: Link findings to tickets                     │
│  + Knowledge base: Check against team coding standards           │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

## Review Dimensions

### Security
- SQL injection, XSS, CSRF
- Authentication and authorization flaws
- Secrets or credentials in code
- Insecure deserialization
- Path traversal
- SSRF

### Performance
- N+1 queries
- Unnecessary memory allocations
- Algorithmic complexity (O(n²) in hot paths)
- Missing database indexes
- Unbounded queries or loops
- Resource leaks

### Correctness
- Edge cases (empty input, null, overflow)
- Race conditions and concurrency issues
- Error handling and propagation
- Off-by-one errors
- Type safety

### Maintainability
- Naming clarity
- Single responsibility
- Duplication
- Test coverage
- Documentation for non-obvious logic

## Output

```markdown
## Code Review: [PR title or file]

### Summary
[1-2 sentence overview of the changes and overall quality]

### Critical Issues
| # | File | Line | Issue | Severity |
|---|------|------|-------|----------|
| 1 | [file] | [line] | [description] | 🔴 Critical |

### Suggestions
| # | File | Line | Suggestion | Category |
|---|------|------|------------|----------|
| 1 | [file] | [line] | [description] | Performance |

### What Looks Good
- [Positive observations]

### Verdict
[Approve / Request Changes / Needs Discussion]
```

## If Connectors Available

If **~~source control** is connected:
- Pull the PR diff automatically from the URL
- Check CI status and test results

If **~~project tracker** is connected:
- Link findings to related tickets
- Verify the PR addresses the stated requirements

If **~~knowledge base** is connected:
- Check changes against team coding standards and style guides

## Tips

1. **Provide context** — "This is a hot path" or "This handles PII" helps me focus.
2. **Specify concerns** — "Focus on security" narrows the review.
3. **Include tests** — I'll check test coverage and quality too.

## Code Review: [PR #518 — validation-result cache by video_id](https://github.com/Khelsutra/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/518)

### Summary
Adds a `video_id`-keyed validation-result cache so an identical-content reupload skips the (minutes-long) validator suite when the validator config is unchanged. The change is well-scoped, thoroughly tested, and CI-green. My adversarial pass found **one real robustness gap**, which I fixed and pushed (`da717e7`).

### Critical Issues
| # | File | Line | Issue | Severity |
|---|------|------|-------|----------|
| — | — | — | None found | — |

### Findings
| # | File | Line | Finding | Category | Status |
|---|------|------|---------|----------|--------|
| 1 | [orchestration.py](backend/orchestration.py:833) | 833 (pre-fix) | `ValidationResult(**r)` replay sat outside the cache `try/except` → a JSON-loadable but schema-incompatible cached row would raise and **fail the job** instead of degrading to fresh validation | 🟠 Correctness/robustness | **✅ Fixed `da717e7`** — guard widened; poisoned-row test added |
| 2 | [base.py](backend/pipeline/validators/base.py:200) | ~200 | Fingerprint hashes the *sport name*, not the sport profile's relevance-config contents (a fallback path only). Same-name profile code change wouldn't invalidate | 🟡 Edge case | Accepted — schema-version escape hatch covers it |
| 3 | [orchestration.py](backend/orchestration.py:802) | 802 | `notify("validating")` fires on a cache hit before the skip | ⚪ Cosmetic | Accepted — reuse is sub-ms |
| 4 | [orchestration.py](backend/orchestration.py:829) | ~829 | On reuse `val_context={}` → obs-report media block degrades to size-only | ⚪ By-design | Accepted — deliberate, keeps reuse ~0-cost |

### What Looks Good
- **Content-hash keying** — `video_id` is the MD5, so a different video (even same filename) never reuses another's verdict; a dedicated test proves it.
- **Fingerprint captures the full validation surface** — the whole `validation.*` block (so a new knob is covered automatically) *plus* `sport` (caught by reading `relevance.py`, which keys its court profile off it — a field outside `validation`) *plus* the validator set *plus* a bumpable schema version.
- **Dedicated-table design** minimizes blast radius — one read site, one write site; no threading the fingerprint through `add_video`'s four callers or the cloud-staging path.
- **Security clean** — parameterized SQL, no secrets, no PII in the cache, no path use of `video_id`.
- **Guardrails enforced by tests** — config change *and* sport change force re-validation; `force=true` bypasses + refreshes; cached FAIL replays without re-decode.
- **Migration v10** follows the established `CREATE ... IF NOT EXISTS` re-runnable pattern; additive-only.

### Verdict
**✅ Approve** — after the applied hardening (`da717e7`). CI on `4008742` was **11/11 green**; it's re-running on `da717e7` (small error-handling refinement + test). Locally: full suite **1853p/1s @ 85.81%**, process-path suites **156p**, cache suite **11p** all green post-fix.

**Note:** this is my own change, so treat this as a rigorous self-review, not independent sign-off. For the billed independent pass, run **`/code-review ultra 518`** (owner-triggered — I can't launch it). One deploy reminder stands: **migration v10 → deploy worker + control-plane together**.
